the DRAM/memory-cost GM headwind **~75-100bps through CY2026** → **"a little bit more than" 100bps, persisting through 2027**. **Two carried theses were downgraded, both on management's own words, neither of which appeared in the sell-side first-takes:** (1) the **"CY27 growth > CY26" commitment** — the single most-cited bull leg on this page since the April call, and the basis for JPM's "only large-cap to put a stake in the ground" framing — became *"in and around $190 billion… translates into a mid-20 tech growth rate, **which is similar to the growth rate of 2026**"*; (2) the **Margem row in the Sinal-vs-gestão table moved ✓ confirma → ⚠ nuança**, because the 62.5% guide beating the 62% Jefferies bogey had been read as the memory headwind easing, and the call says it is bigger and runs a year longer. **One bear leg was contested and one confirmed:** UBS's *"systems backlog is back to normal, so KLA depends on new orders just like peers"* is refuted by a re-disclosed **RPO ~$12.5B** that has "grown pretty consistently," with all live customer conversations on **2H27 deliveries** and 18-24 month lead times on some products; conversely UBS's *"KLAC is the least likely of the Big Three to pursue pricing"* was **confirmed by management on tape** — asked directly why KLA can't do what ASML is doing on EUV pricing, Bren answered *"it's pretty hard to go back to your customers after you've taken orders and start to change prices on those orders."* Also new: full FQ4 model detail (OpEx $682M, op margin 43.7%, incremental op margin 59%, FCF $817M, $876M returned, TTM $3.3B / 28% FCF margin); Sept mix foundry-logic ~73% / memory ~27% (DRAM ~90%) with memory rising into Dec and 1H27; 2H26 ~20% above 1H; **⚠️ a basis conflict logged rather than harmonized** — full-year CY26 foundry/logic given as *"close to 65-ish percent, low to mid-60s"* on the semiconductor-customers-only basis vs the **~82%/~18%** this page carries from the Q3 call, which management explicitly says is a different denominator; **⚠️ the competitive-scale mark moved from ~7x to "6X our nearest rival"** (Snapshot left at ~7x from the Q3 call, both marks now on the page — a casual verbal figure, not a restatement). New forward items added to Catalysts: the CY27 ">"→"≈" revision test, and a **2030 target-model reset** that management effectively pre-announced (the model's $215B WFE / $1.4T semi market is arriving ~4 years early; assumptions restated as semi +11% CAGR, WFE +12%, KLA share +150bps ±25bps). Also logged: management sees **greenfield flash/NAND investment in 2027**, which contests the page's carried JPM view of no NAND greenfield until CY28. Cross-filed [[ASML]] (the pricing mirror), [[AMAT]], [[INTC]], [[SAMSUNG]], [[AVGO]], [[SPCX]], [themes/semicap-wfe](themes/semicap-wfe.md), [themes/hbm-memory](themes/hbm-memory.md), [themes/cowos-packaging](themes/cowos-packaging.md). |

Logged as an open contradiction for the FY2026 share data to settle rather than resolved either way. Forward-dated by TER: *"the effective share change in 2027… gradual, socket-by-socket"*, with its merchant-GPU first order **shipped in the June quarter** and **correlation completed at a second hyperscaler** — so the challenge to the GPU-test incumbency is a CY27 volume question, consistent with Hon Precision's evidence (07-30) that the SLT incumbency is attacked but not taken. **Second item, TAM-supportive:** management-sourced test-intensity numbers that independently corroborate BofA's 7-8% band from a different derivation — test as a share of **semi-capex** at **~4% (2023) → 7% (2025) → 8% (first five months of 2026)**, settling **7-9% (most likely 7-8%)**, implying **ATE TAM ≥$20B** on WFE approaching **~$250B** by end of decade, with *"the trend reversed in 2024 — test is now outpacing fab equipment."* ⚠️ Two caveats carried deliberately: TER itself allows the ratio **could revert to 6-7% in 2027** on the WFE→ATE lag (a caveat the sell-side "sustaining at 7-8%" framing drops), and this **semi-capex denominator is not the same ratio** as the ~1% test-TAM-÷-semiconductor-revenue metric UBS uses — both now on the page, not harmonized. Cross-filed [[TER]], [themes/semicap-wfe](themes/semicap-wfe.md), [themes/hbm-memory](themes/hbm-memory.md). |

ASE positioned as **"the primary partner for TSMC and the largest wafer-level packaging capacity provider outside of TSMC, much bigger than Amkor or Intel,"** with privileged access to leading-edge technologies **including COUPE**; panel-level packaging (310×310mm) ramps 1Q27, with CPO and EMIB contributing from 2027 and more meaningfully in 2028. **Separately, and materially for the margin debate: Chipstrat decomposes TSMC's recent gross-margin expansion as "gross margin rose 9.1 points, and manufacturing performance explained 3.2 of those points. The other 5.9 points? PRICING" — roughly two-thirds price, not cost, against management framing that points to utilisation and cost reduction. TSMC "historically played it safe, erring on the side of undercharging… That's changing"** (Lyons, 2026-07-31). Pricing pressure is real; the volume threat slips. ⚠️ **Logged as a reported claim, not a confirmed plan: "Elon Musk designed Tesla's China business to be EASILY SEPARATED from its U.S. business because of geopolitical tensions. It might also come in handy IF HE PROCEEDS WITH A SPACEX MERGER"** (WSJ, 2026-07-31) — the first public tie between the China-separability structure and [[SPCX]] merger mechanics, and live now that SpaceX is listed. large-package ATC handlers (>120x150mm) carry a 20-25% price premium shipping from Oct 2026, with customer interest going from 1-2 to "almost every one" and a possible 20-30% of AI shipments in 1H27 rising to >50% in 2H27; customer thermal roadmaps target ~10kW in 2027-28; CPO insertion-4 co-test handlers ship on schedule in October as high-ASP option value; and within Hon's AI exposure management expects ASICs to SURPASS GPUs in 2027** (JPM · Huang/Tsai, 2026-07-30). ⚠️ **Competitive warning worth carrying: Hon has completed only the FIRST stage of qualification on the Nvidia GPU SLT project, has NO confirmed GPU SLT order wins, and names COST STRUCTURE as its shortcoming vs its peer — the incumbent position in GPU SLT is being attacked but has not yet been taken.** Also: **China FT handler orders +>60% y/y and SLT +>80% y/y with Hon at 90-100% share of China high-end**, and a named list of OSAT capacity projects including **US facilities for both a Taiwan and a Korea OSAT** — onshoring is real and multi-vendor. | 2026-07-31 | ⭐ STX | (wiki-ingest) — the memory shortage is now visible in hyperscaler capex and named to include hard drives, which is the cleanest demand read this page has had.** No rating or PT superseded. ⚠️ **The most useful item is a framing one: JPM ran its AAPL and STX post-earnings takeaways on the SAME analyst call (2026-07-31, 10am ET) — Apple's memory-cost problem and Seagate's memory-cycle upside are two sides of a single shortage. The mechanism: constrained and expensive flash pushes nearline capacity back toward disk, so the tightness that compresses Apple's gross margin supports HDD pricing and mix.** **The direct datapoint: [[AMZN]] named "inflated prices right now on some of the components like memory and HARD DRIVES AND SSDs" on its Q2 call, and attributed the whole $20bn increase in its FY26 capex guide (~$200bn → ~$220bn) to higher memory costs** (JPM · Cardoso, 2026-07-30). Customer-side corroboration: **Apple guided Sept-Q GM down with memory accounting for >100% of the sequential compression and said "for September, we expect to pay EVEN HIGHER memory costs"; UBS models DRAM and NAND ASPs +~30% q/q into December.** **Duration and mechanics from the memory complex: Samsung disclosed 60-70% LTA coverage with a price-cut FLOOR of sub-5% q/q against increases open at 10-20%+ on 5-year rolling tenors; Kioxia is "on track toward 50% LTA volume coverage for CY28" and guides CY27 "demand anticipated to exceed supply"; TrendForce July DRAM contract price +low-teens % m/m = 30-50% q/q, FLAT for NAND** (BofA · Woo/Shen/Arya, 2026-07-31). ⚠️ **The DRAM/NAND divergence in July is worth watching for this page specifically, since flat NAND contract pricing is a weaker push toward disk than the DRAM move implies.** BofA's sector read: hyperscalers are **"seemingly not bothered by rising prices of chips/memory,"** with Korean results implying tight supply and elevated pricing **"at least through 2027."** Cross-filed [[AAPL]], [[AMZN]], [[WDC]], [[MU]], [[KIOXIA]], [[SNDK]], [themes/hbm-memory](themes/hbm-memory.md). |

| 2026-07-31 | ⭐ SNDK | (wiki-ingest) — Kioxia's print gives the closest NAND read-across, and the message is that price is doing all the work while consumer demand contributes nothing.** No rating or PT superseded. **Kioxia missed modestly (revenue ¥1.76tn vs ¥1.84tn; OP ¥1.27tn vs ¥1.372tn consensus, OPM 75%; Sep-Q OP guided ¥1.9tn vs ¥1.95tn) against a bar already marked down — the stock went limit up pre-print, fell after hours, then recovered to ~+3%. But ASP was +70% Q/Q LIKE-FOR-LIKE on only low-single-digit bit growth: the entire revenue move is price, and the volume restraint is deliberate. CY26 bit growth high-teens % "in line with Samsung guidance"; CY27 "demand anticipated to exceed supply."** ⚠️ **Structurally the most important disclosure: Kioxia is "on track toward 50% LTA volume coverage for CY28" — half of 2028 output pre-committed. Combined with Samsung's 60-70% LTA coverage (with a sub-5% q/q price-cut floor against increases open at 10-20%+, on 5-year rolling tenors), NAND/DRAM price formation now runs largely through contracts rather than spot, which changes what spot prints signal.** Capital return: **¥800bn buyback (30mn shares = 5.5% of shares out, 3 Aug → 30 Oct) plus a 3-for-1 split effective 1 Oct, funded from ¥187bn net cash and ¥827bn post-capex FCF.** ⚠️ **The key risk read for SNDK: agentic AI is "a primary growth catalyst" and server NAND is benefiting, but SMARTPHONE NAND DEMAND IS FLAT vs CY25 and PC NAND A SLIGHT DECLINE on higher BOM costs — the cycle is carried entirely by datacenter.** Corroboration and a timing caveat from [[LRCX]]: **data centres increasingly demand SLC/TLC (not QLC) NAND for AI memory stacks — more expensive, more capacity, "a must" — but teed up for '28-'30, with NAND WFE actually DOWN 1H'26 vs 1H'25 in dollars.** ⚠️ **Positioning, logged so the move is not misread: SNDK was among the largest holdings in the force-sold Situational Awareness book and among the best performers on the unwind (+26% on 07-30) — the July drawdown was substantially forced selling, but the same fact confirms concentrated single-holder risk now transferred to a very different holder.** SNDK sits on BofA's **second-wave** AI re-entry list of names still 30-50% off highs. **MS SNDK 2Q callback with Joe Moore diarised 2026-08-06, 9am ET.** Cross-filed [[KIOXIA]], [[MU]], [[SAMSUNG]], [[STX]], [[WDC]], [[LRCX]], [themes/hbm-memory](themes/hbm-memory.md). |
| 2026-07-31 | ⭐ SMCI | (wiki-ingest) — SMCI named as a direct beneficiary of NVDA's new GPU backstop programs, with a caveat that matters more here than for peers.** No rating or PT superseded. **The mechanism: NVDA is giving neoclouds a MINIMUM REVENUE GUARANTEE on GPU capacity they cannot lease out, in exchange for a share of revenue above a threshold; FIRMUS and SHARON AI named as early partners. GS: "These backstop programs should broaden out compute availability to end customers by supporting continued neocloud growth. We view continued robust neocloud demand as positive for leading AI server vendors DELL and SMCI"** (Murphy/Ng, AI Project Pulse July 2026, 2026-07-31). ⚠️ **Given SMCI's heavier neocloud mix, the caveat lands harder here: the backstop moves demand risk from the neocloud to NVDA, sustaining ORDER FLOW regardless of whether the capacity is actually let. JPM: Korea's l/s ratio has retraced to 2.8x (80th %-ile) from 6x, with net exposures ~60% off highs (vs KOSPI −40%) — but STILL +4.5z ABOVE THE 2018 AVERAGE. | 2026-07-31 | ⭐ SAMSUNG | (wiki-ingest) — the post-2Q-call detail, and the single most reusable finding is that LTAs are asymmetric collars, not price locks.** ⚠️ **THESIS-DRIFT logged on the valuation basis: BofA raised 2026-28 EPS by 3-5% (on 2-3% higher memory volume/price assumptions and more favourable LTA terms) but CUT the target 2027-28E P/E from ~~10x~~ → **9x**, leaving the PO unchanged at W550,000** — near the top of the previous peak-cycle band of 7-10x (Woo, 2026-07-31). **The OP path is net-new and large: 2Q OP W89.5tn with DRAM OPM ~80% / NAND ~70% even after special bonuses; DRAM ASP guided +23% q/q in 3Q and +8% in 4Q (after +91%/+45% in 1Q/2Q), taking quarterly OP above W100tn (annual W400tn+) in 3Q/4Q26; then ASP flat-to-slightly-down through 2027-28 under LTAs with annual OP still climbing to W500tn+ on volume and yield. Capex W78-100tn p.a. 2026-28; FCF W200tn+ (2026) → W300tn+ (2027-28).** ⚠️ **The trade being described deserves flagging: Samsung is deliberately giving price back "per AI server content increase" rather than defending ASP — a volume/share strategy, not price maximisation.** **THE LTA MECHANICS, which invert the common bear reading: 60-70% of Samsung memory sales go under LTA, but with a price-cut FLOOR of sub-5% q/q against price increases broadly open at 10-20%+, on mostly 5-year ROLLING tenors where a 6th year can be added as the 1st completes. High LTA coverage is therefore a downside collar with uncapped upside — bullish for realised ASP stability, not a cap on it.** Spot corroboration: **TrendForce July DRAM contract price +low-teens % m/m = 30-50% q/q, FLAT for NAND** (note the divergence); China domestic smartphone shipments bottoming at **17mn units in June vs a normal 25-30mn**; and minimal spot impact from China local capacity because **"China-branded memory [is] not commonly traded in the spot market."** Capital return: buyback framed as **"when, not if"** on low treasury holdings, with 50% of FCF committed (Edward Kim, Head of Korean Equities); **no immediate ADR plan**; **foundry near breakeven in 2H ex-bonus on the 2nm ramp and new US customers.** Third-party mark logged: **KIS PT raised to KRW 650,000**, above BofA's W550,000 (@jukan05, 07-30). Stock **+21%** on the print; **KOSPI +18% overnight with SK Hynix +30% limit-up and Samsung +27%** as the Situational Awareness book cleared. Adjacent corroboration filed: **SEMCO 2Q OP more than doubled y/y on AI MLCC/substrates, annual OP W2tn in 2026 → W5tn+ in 2027, with prepayments funding capacity — and all three top MLCC makers (SEMCO, Murata, Taiyo Yuden) have raised prices.** Cross-filed [[SKHYNIX]], [[KIOXIA]], [[MU]], [[AAPL]], [themes/hbm-memory](themes/hbm-memory.md). |

Monetization milestones net-new: **ARPU +36% y/y to $6.18; >$1M revenue per employee; active advertisers +70% y/y; Max advertisers +60% q/q with Max revenue +150% q/q; ad revenue +64% against a 23pt tougher comp.** Stock **−23% on 07-31**. | 2026-07-31 | ⭐ NBIS | (wiki-ingest) — three items that change how neocloud risk should be underwritten: a new listed comp coming, a supplier-written demand collar, and confirmation that July's drawdown was forced selling.** No rating or PT superseded. **① Nscale (UK, NVDA-backed) is preparing an IPO with GS and JPM, investor day in New York the week of Aug 3, potentially listing this fall. Bankers have floated ~$25bn vs a $14.6bn March valuation, and the company projects revenue TRIPLING to $30BN in 2027 — more than analysts expect [[CRWV]] to generate next year** (The Information · Valida Pau, 2026-07-30). ⚠️ **The specific risk to that number: the West Virginia complex acquired from American Intelligence & Power still needs "tens of billions" of financing AND HAS NO ANNOUNCED TENANT.** Nscale is also buying **Anyscale for $1.6bn**, explicitly mirroring CoreWeave's $1.7bn Weights & Biases deal before its own IPO; has raised **>$6bn** from 8090 Industries, Citadel and Jane Street; and added **Sheryl Sandberg and Nick Clegg** to the board. ⚠️ Tape context against the timing: **CRWV −25% over the past month; Sharon AI −42% over the past month** after being +60% since its February IPO; **Lambda delayed its own listing; Crusoe is talking to bankers.** **② The structural item, and the most important one: NVDA is writing GPU BACKSTOP PROGRAMS for neoclouds — a MINIMUM REVENUE GUARANTEE on GPU capacity if the neocloud cannot lease it out, in exchange for a share of revenue above a threshold. FIRMUS and SHARON AI named as early partners** (GS · Murphy/Ng, AI Project Pulse July 2026, 2026-07-31). ⚠️ **This is a collar written by the chip supplier on its own customers' demand risk: it truncates neocloud downside, caps upside, and means reported neocloud capacity is no longer a clean read on independent end-demand.** GS views it as broadening compute availability and positive for [[DELL]]/[[SMCI]]. Also net-new and a genuinely specific customer reference: **Revolut pre-trained PRAGMA on H1000 GPUs in the Nebius cloud, reporting 65% more fraud cases caught, 2.3x better credit-default identification accuracy and 41% more relevant product recommendations.** **③ NBIS was among the largest holdings in the Situational Awareness book force-sold to Citadel, and was the best performer on the unwind at +26-27% on 07-30** ("NBIS +27%; BE +27%; SNDK +26%; CRWV +22%" — TMT Breakout). ⚠️ Reading both directions: July's drawdown was substantially a forced-selling artefact rather than a fundamental re-rating, **but** the same fact confirms NBIS carried concentrated single-holder risk, now transferred to a holder with very different behaviour. Fund detail: **~$20bn AUM, +400% in 2026, up to 4x levered, −67% in July; ~$16bn public book sold in one block; ~$5bn Anthropic private stake retained.** Cross-filed [[CRWV]], [[NVDA]], [[DELL]], [[SMCI]], [[ANTHROPIC]], [themes/ai-datacenter-power](themes/ai-datacenter-power.md). |
| 2026-07-31 | ⭐ MU | (wiki-ingest) — the LTA mechanics were disclosed, and they invert how rising contract coverage should be read.** No rating or PT superseded. **The core finding: Samsung said 60-70% of its memory sales will be under LTA, but with ASYMMETRIC terms — a price-cut FLOOR of sub-5% q/q against price increases broadly open at 10-20%+, on mostly 5-YEAR ROLLING tenors where a 6th year can be added as the 1st completes. Kioxia separately said it is "on track toward 50% LTA VOLUME COVERAGE FOR CY28 with key customers."** ⚠️ **These are DOWNSIDE COLLARS WITH UNCAPPED UPSIDE, not price locks — so high LTA coverage is bullish for realised ASP stability rather than a cap on it, which is the opposite of the usual late-cycle reading. It also means spot prints are a progressively weaker signal of realised pricing as coverage rises.** **Pricing evidence net-new: Kioxia's Jun-Q ASP +70% q/q LIKE-FOR-LIKE on only low-single-digit bit growth; Samsung DRAM OPM ~80% / NAND OPM ~70% in 2Q with DRAM ASP guided +23% q/q in 3Q and +8% in 4Q (after +91%/+45% in 1Q/2Q); TrendForce July DRAM contract price +low-teens % m/m = 30-50% q/q — but FLAT FOR NAND** (BofA · Woo/Shen/Arya, 2026-07-31). ⚠️ **The DRAM/NAND divergence in the July contract print is the detail to carry; the two are not moving together.** ⚠️ **A ceiling on ASP upside also disclosed: Samsung is explicitly trading price for content — ASP guided flat-to-slightly-down through 2027-28 under LTAs and "chipmakers' strategic control (e.g., decent price cut per AI server content increase)" — a share/volume strategy, not price maximisation, with annual OP still rising to W500tn+ on volume and yield.** Duration: **Samsung sees an "ongoing DRAM supply shortage due to AI, EVEN WITH new capacity expansion through 2027-2028"; Kioxia guides CY27 "demand anticipated to exceed supply."** **Spot drivers into August: restocking orders, Tier-2 OEMs resuming Sept/4Q launches despite high cost, and China domestic smartphone shipments bottoming at 17MN UNITS IN JUNE vs a normal 25-30mn.** On China supply: **"minimal impact… China-branded memory NOT commonly traded in the spot market."** ⚠️ **Best available confirmation of pricing power, because it shows up in the buyers' own numbers: [[AMZN]] raised FY26 capex ~$200bn → ~$220bn with the increment attributed EXPLICITLY to higher memory costs; [[META]] attributed its raise to component and memory pricing; [[AAPL]] said memory accounts for >100% of its Sept-Q GM compression, with UBS modelling DRAM/NAND ASPs +~30% q/q into December. BofA: hyperscalers are "seemingly NOT BOTHERED by rising prices of chips/memory," with Korean results implying tight supply and elevated pricing "at least through 2027."** Demand mix caveat: **agentic AI and server NAND are carrying the cycle — smartphone NAND demand is FLAT vs CY25 and PC NAND a slight DECLINE on higher BOM costs.** Cross-filed [[SAMSUNG]], [[SKHYNIX]], [[KIOXIA]], [[SNDK]], [[STX]], [[WDC]], [[AAPL]], [[AMZN]], [themes/hbm-memory](themes/hbm-memory.md). |

| 2026-07-31 | ⭐ META | (wiki-ingest) — the capex debate now has two houses arguing opposite conclusions from the same print, and the contrast with [[AMZN]] is the most instructive fact of the week.** No rating or PT superseded. **① Redburn (Dom Ball via Falkiner, BUY, PT $1,000) is the only house on this page forecasting a capex PEAK: total capex peaking at $142BN IN 2027 then FALLING to $131BN IN 2028 — explicitly "15%/30% BELOW CONS." The mechanism is supply, not demand: an internal compute model projecting ~6GW OF OVER-CAPACITY BY 2030, hence "META HAS OVER-BUILT [and] a 'YEAR OF EFFICIENCY 2.0' WILL COME."** The strategic leg is the pivot from a trailing consumer LLM to a leading **SMB LLM / "business-in-a-box,"** with three new evidence points: **Zuckerberg on the call ("we'd like to build this into a business-in-a-box service that can help you start and run a whole business using Meta's platforms"); Alexandr Wang confirming on 26 July that "Meta will be launching a HARNESS" in 2H26 (a layer letting the model ACT, not just answer); and Muse Spark 1.1 leading on video/image-to-website generation.** Sized at **~0 → ~30% of Meta SMBs by 2030 at ~$78/month = ~$2bn-$80bn cumulative 2026-2030**, inside a **~$209bn opportunity at 45% IRR** on ~250m business distribution. On compute monetisation Redburn sides with Zuckerberg — **"the margin on selling intelligence is higher than raw compute"** — preferring a diversified AI-cloud offering to "a concentrated neo-cloud business selling to a handful of buyers." ⚠️ **The paired trade is a franchise call: BUY META vs SHOP (downgraded to Neutral) because the same SMB LLM "IN-SOURCES Shopify's most underappreciated engine, putting ~50% of its US volumes at risk and potentially HALVING group growth," on a ~90% ad-revenue correlation and SHOP at 52x PE** — see [[SHOP]]. **② The buy side read the identical print the opposite way, and not on magnitude but on narrative: "META CONFUSION… vision remains unclear. Capex was raised +$2bn at the midpoint, LESS than expected… TOO MANY OPTIONS ARE BEING KEPT OPEN? IT CREATES ROI QUESTIONS," with the print judged to have "FAILED TO… INSPIRE" next to the hyperscalers' ROI disclosures. Stock −8% on 13x core P/E** (BofA · Fenske/Liu via Villani, 2026-07-30). ⚠️ **The comparison worth carrying forward as a standing lesson: Meta raised capex LESS than expected and de-rated 8%, while AMZN raised capex $20bn MORE than expected and rallied 14%. The market is paying for ROI narrative, not spending restraint.** **③ Reconciled capex facts: C2Q26 capex +57% q/q / +83% y/y to $31bn; 1H26 $51bn (+66% y/y); FY26 NARROWED to $130-145bn from $125-145bn — a $5bn raise to the LOW END ONLY, implying 90%+ y/y growth. No reason given, but Meta had earlier attributed higher 2026 capex to component and memory pricing, so the raise may be cost rather than capacity.** Management declined a 2027 figure but framed plans around **"preserv[ing] flexibility for server decisions in 2028 and beyond"** — which, read carefully, is not inconsistent with Redburn's over-capacity thesis. Meta frames internal silicon as **"supply-chain leverage"** (JPM · Cardoso, 2026-07-30). ⚠️ **Logged as an unverified CLAIM, not a datapoint: a report that Meta "has already committed almost $700 billion in future AI spending" with aggregate hyperscaler off-balance-sheet commitments at $3tn+, "double in one quarter" (@zerohedge, 2026-07-30) — needs confirmation against filings before use.** Cross-filed [[SHOP]], [[AMZN]], [[MSFT]], [[GOOG]], [themes/hyperscaler-capex](themes/hyperscaler-capex.md). |

This is the single most consequential update this page has taken.** ⚠️ **THESIS DRIFT — SUPERSEDED VALUES, retained here in full: (a) 2027 AI-accelerator SAM ~~US$70-80bn~~ → **US$80bn**; (b) MediaTek's 2027 share target ~~10-15%~~ → **15-20%**; (c) implied 2027 ASIC revenue ~~US$7-12bn~~ → **US$12-16bn**; (d) 2026 ASIC revenue ~~"exceed US$1bn in FY26, ~US$2bn in Q4 FY26 alone"~~ → **"more than US$2bn" for 2026**, production from Q4'26.** The raise landed at the **top** of the bull range: Fubon's 15-20% call (07-30) and MS's expected raise to US$12-15bn (Chan, 07-26) were both vindicated — logged as a **win for the variant view this page has been carrying since June.** ⚠️ **A SLIP also logged: TPU v9 "Humufish" high-volume production now guided to EARLY 2028, vs JPM's prior model of late 2027** — management reframed it as a positive ("the first and second ASIC will be in production at the same year, driving significant share gains"), which is a real argument but does not undo the slip. **The EMIB-T debate moved materially and in MediaTek's favour: asked directly, management said "Yes this is true, we are more committed to EMIB-T" — the strongest first-party EMIB-T endorsement anywhere on this wiki, and it resolves in the affirmative the question MS listed as item (2) into the call. Counterweight logged the same day: Unimicron says Intel EMIB-T will not reach full mass production before 2027 (@jukan05, 07-31).** ⚠️ **BASIS GUARDRAIL ADDED, and it is the most reusable thing here: the US$80bn SAM is ACCELERATOR-ONLY — it excludes HBM and network switch. Management confirmed Google buys the HBM directly, not MediaTek, and Jefferies' checks say saving that HBM margin is "one of the key reasons Google is moving more to MTK." MediaTek ASIC dollars therefore must NOT be compared gross against an AVGO figure that may embed HBM pass-through.** Genuine surprise: **400G SerDes ready 2H27** (Fubon: "a huge surprise"), timed for TPU v10 in 2029 — ⚠️ **with a sourcing inconsistency flagged rather than resolved: prepared remarks referenced 448G (BofA cites 448G) while the Q&A said 400G (Fubon/JPM); the generation label is unsettled.** Also net-new: co-packaged copper today → CPO via TSMC COUPE later, an own-IP rack design including **NVLink Fusion**, and the explicit admission that **"beyond 400G SerDes will need to move to optical."** Pricing precision worth keeping: the hikes are **defensive** — "we are not increasing price to increase GM… we are just looking to maintain GM." **PTs: GS RAISED NT$6,800 → NT$7,000; JPM NT$5,300, BofA NT$5,230, Fubon NT$6,000 all held. JPM says consensus FY27 EPS should rise 5-10%.** BofA now asserts co-development with **xAI, tape-out 4Q27**, plus Meta and Toyota/Denso optionality — logged as an assertion, **not** confirmed: management said only "we have several, we will provide details when we can," and JPM independently guesses SpaceX. | 2026-07-31 | ⭐ LRCX | (wiki-ingest) — a first-party post-Q2 follow-up call carrying an on-the-record rebuttal of a competitor's ALD Moly claim, plus the first quantification of the backside-power opportunity.** No rating or PT superseded (company call). **Net-new: ① DRAM is guided to be the FASTEST-GROWING WFE segment in FY26, outgrowing leading-edge foundry logic, with Lam expecting to OUTGROW overall DRAM WFE — on conductor-etch leadership, Akara adoption doubling y/y since launch, >70% diffusion-barrier share, plus dry photoresist/HBM/TSV kickers. FY27 has DRAM and foundry logic "a lot closer," NAND "a distant third." ② Backside power delivery sized at ~$1BN PER 100K WAFERS, playing to Lam's backside-metals ALD strength. ③ ⚠️ A DIRECT REBUTTAL, logged as contested rather than settled: management "firmly rejects a competitor's claim of ALD Moly wins in foundry logic," holding that NAND adopts Moly FIRST and that Lam is the largest Moly player in NAND production. ④ Gross margin target of ~52% → mid-50s over "several years" on footprint optimisation, strategic pricing with new customers, and Akara/Vantex mix — explicitly contrasted against KLA's more subdued expansion.** ⚠️ **Two quality caveats that cut against the headline and are the most useful things here: (a) the CSBG beat was LOW-QUALITY — +43% and 15-16% above expectations, but driven by SPARES (customer pre-buying amid back-end constraints) and Reliant upside rather than upgrades, and CSBG is guided FLATTISH for the next two quarters with September's uptick coming from systems instead; (b) NAND WFE is DOWN 1H'26 vs 1H'25 in dollars — the structural SLC/TLC-for-AI-memory-stack demand ("a must," more expensive, more capacity) is teed up for '28-'30, NOT 2026-27.** Anyone quoting NAND recovery off this call needs that split. Independent corroboration filed: **LRCX inventory days at a 19-QUARTER LOW** — demand drawing down inventory rather than building it — with "visibility into new fab builds significantly higher than normal" into 2028, and **both KLAC and LRCX raising 2026 advanced-packaging expectations** (DB · Weathers, 2026-07-30/31; MKSI PT $380→$360 Buy, ONTO Buy $350). Sector calibration: **2027 likely a fourth consecutive year of WFE outgrowth; KLAC's 2026 WFE outlook raised to the low-$150bn range with backlog +59% y/y; test intensity at 7-8% of WFE = a $14bn+ ATE opportunity at $200bn WFE** (BofA · Arya, 2026-07-30). Stock **+18%** on 07-30, and LRCX sits in the first tranche of buy-side AI re-entry alongside AMAT and ASML. Watch the **Flash Memory Summit** for NAND colour. | 2026-07-31 | ⭐ LITE | (wiki-ingest) — Lumentum has reversed course on single-sourcing InP substrates, which is both a supply-security signal and the retirement of a bear argument.** No rating or PT superseded. **The item: Lumentum has signed a long-term InP substrate supply agreement with AXT — despite having, only months ago, expressed confidence in a ~7-YEAR SUMITOMO agreement.** ⚠️ **A 7-year incumbent agreement proved insufficient, and the Lumentum CEO has been "open recently about how they need to MULTI SOURCE AGGRESSIVELY and secure as much as possible"** (AXT 2Q26 call, primary, via buyside notes, 2026-07-31). **The inference worth carrying: substrate COST is not the binding concern — AVAILABILITY is.** Supporting context: **AXT is shipping record InP wafer volumes to both LITE and COHR "and they still want more"; AXT is ahead of schedule doubling InP capacity in 2026 with the InP revenue opportunity tripling by end-2026 and ~$130M/quarter exiting 2027 (~$520M annualised); backlog >$100M; and "demand outpaces supply, no matter how fast they add capacity."** Demand mechanism: **800G→1.6T with MORE LASERS PER TRANSCEIVER, higher-spec 6-inch substrates, and hyperscalers advancing toward NPO and CPO.** ⚠️ **One boundary on the read-through: AXT still lacks US export permits, with ~$66M of Q3-realisable revenue not requiring one — a US permit unlock would be upside no one is currently forecasting.** Positioning: LITE is on BofA's **second-wave** AI re-entry list of names still 30-50% off highs (with GLW, MRVL, SNDK, KLAC, INTC); named among JPM's beneficiaries of **~$750bn 2026 hyperscaler capex.** **Events: JPM COHR + LITE callback 2026-08-14 10am ET; LITE at the JPM HW & Semis Management Access Forum 08-12.** Cross-filed [[AXTI]], [[COHR]], [[GLW]], [[POET]], [themes/optical-cpo](themes/optical-cpo.md). |

Sep-Q guided to revenue ¥2.39tn / OP ¥1.9tn vs ¥1.95tn consensus (¥3.16tn OP for the Apr-Sep half).** Jefferies argues the miss is optical against buy-side expectations that had already been marked down — the stock went **limit up pre-print**, dumped after hours, then recovered to ~+3% (O'Mara / Wong, 2026-07-31). **Net-new capital return: ¥800bn buyback, up to 30mn shares = 5.5% of shares out, 3 Aug → 30 Oct 2026; 3-for-1 stock split effective 1 Oct 2026 — funded from a net cash position of ¥187bn and ¥827bn of post-capex FCF.** **The pricing datapoint is the one to carry: ASP +70% q/q like-for-like on only low-single-digit bit growth — the whole revenue move is price, and the volume restraint is deliberate.** Cycle guidance net-new: **CY26 bit growth high-teens %, explicitly "in line with Samsung guidance"; CY27 "demand anticipated to exceed supply."** ⚠️ **The structurally important disclosure: "on track toward our 50% LTA volume coverage for CY28 with key customers."** Half of 2028 output pre-committed under LTAs — read alongside Samsung's 60-70% LTA coverage disclosed the same week, this is a NAND/DRAM market whose price formation now runs largely through contracts rather than spot, which changes what spot prints mean as a signal. But the two are compatible — MediaTek's claim is yield on track for v9 MP in 2028; Unimicron's is no full-scale MP before 2027. What is no longer disputed is the demand rationale: Unimicron says customers switch from CoWoS for LOWER COST and IMPROVED PERFORMANCE, and expects them to stay on EMIB-T for THREE PRODUCT GENERATIONS** (@jukan05, 2026-07-31). Quarter shape added: **INTC server ASP +48% y/y on strengthening agentic-AI demand, but server units DOWN ~6% q/q and supply "bounded by wafers, substrates," with no material new Intel 3 supply until Q4 when 18A ramps in higher volume across desktops and servers** (BofA · Arya, 2026-07-30) — pricing and mix doing the work while volume is supply-capped. Positioning: **INTC +12.16% on the Situational Awareness unwind day**, with desk commentary attributing the prior drawdown to that forced selling despite Intel having "reported the best quarter of the earnings season"; Intel sits on BofA's **second-wave** AI re-entry list. | 2026-07-31 | ⭐ IFX | (wiki-ingest) — a 300mm wafer shortage call that reads as a 2027 input-cost headwind for Infineon, alongside confirmation that analog end-demand is recovering.** No rating or PT superseded (read-across). **The call: DB upgraded Siltronic to BUY, PT €100 (from €67.95), arguing "the wave of demand from agentic AI is now set to TIP THE 300MM WAFER INDUSTRY INTO SHORTAGE CONDITIONS, STARTING WITH EPITAXIAL WAFERS, BUT WITH POLISHED WAFERS SET TO FOLLOW"** (Sanders, 2026-07-30). **The quantified core: 300mm industry utilisation heading to the MID-90s % in H2-26, which "points to SPOT PRICING HEADING ABOVE LTA-AGREED PRICING on 300mm over the next 12 months," and makes LTA renewals from 2027 a positive driver for producers.** ⚠️ **Spot-above-LTA is the inversion signal and it cuts against Infineon: as a large 300mm substrate buyer, Infineon faces an input-cost headwind into its 2027 LTA renewals — structurally the same pass-through-lag that just compressed [[QCOM]]'s Q4 margin guide (cost inflation arriving before price increases land). The offsetting positive is that the same call confirms mainstream/analog demand destruction is ending.** Also worth noting the mechanism DB identifies for why wafers lagged: **"the AI spending boom has pretty much passed [the wafer industry] by so far given low wafer area consumed by market leaders such as Nvidia"** — it is agentic-AI volume, not accelerator area, that tightens the market. Demand-side corroboration from a second house: **analog trends improving, with [[TXN]] citing accelerating industrial recovery and auto inventory replenishment and [[NXPI]] seeing improving auto drivers "although western tier-one customers are not yet restocking"** (BofA · Arya, 2026-07-30). Sentiment: **JPM flagging "EU Semis — Finally Seeing Green" with a buy-side bar being set on Infineon into the print** (Silver, 2026-07-30), against a tape where the BofA TMT Momentum basket had its **worst month ever (−51%) then its best day ever (+24%)**, with 80-90% historical follow-through over 5/30/90 days. Cross-filed [[TXN]], [[NXPI]], [[ON]], [[QCOM]], [[ASML]], [themes/semicap-wfe](themes/semicap-wfe.md). |
| 2026-07-31 | ⭐ GOOG | (wiki-ingest) — a new Gemini-optimised chip, the first TPU system-sale revenue recognition (with the accounting warning that follows from it), and Google as the named cause of Reddit's de-rating.** No rating or PT superseded. **① The scoop: Google is developing a new chip designed to run Gemini models FAR MORE EFFICIENTLY; Reuters, Bloomberg and CNBC followed, and the reporting was "credited with helping lift Alphabet shares 3%"** (The Information · Woo/Liu, 2026-07-31). **② ⚠️ THE MOST IMPORTANT BASIS POINT ADDED TO THIS PAGE: Google began recognising TPU SYSTEM-SALE REVENUE for the first time this quarter, "with the vast majority expected in 2027" (JPM · Cardoso) — and MS warns those sales book GROSS while AWS books Bedrock 3P consumption NET. Ex-TPU, MS estimates Google Cloud added only $3-4bn q/q, BELOW AWS and close to Azure's ~$3.9bn, despite Google Cloud adding the most headline revenue dollars (~$4.74bn vs AWS ~$4.65bn). AWS added more EBIT dollars ($2.5bn vs $2.2bn) off the smaller revenue add, because "TPU sales have a structurally lower margin." Barclays adds that GCP's largest headline acceleration (+21pp vs AWS +9pp, Azure +4pp) also benefits from a smaller base and an easier comp.** Net: **Google's cloud line now mixes a services business and a hardware business at different margins, with the hardware piece scaling into 2027 — so revenue-growth comparisons against AWS and Azure will become LESS meaningful over time, not more.** **③ Capex: C2Q26 +26% q/q and +100% y/y to $45bn (~60% servers); FY26 guidance RAISED to $195-205BN from $180-190BN = 115%+ y/y or ~+$105BN in dollars, the largest dollar increase of any hyperscaler, with a significant 2027 increase reiterated. ⚠️ Note the reason differs from peers — Google attributed it to "an ACCELERATION IN THE DELIVERY OF CAPACITY," whereas AMZN's $20bn raise was attributed to memory costs and META's to component/memory pricing. Cloud backlog +$50bn q/q to $514bn, the largest of the four.** **④ Vendor-financed AI capacity is becoming a category worth tracking rather than a set of one-offs: Google is reportedly providing a CREDIT BACKSTOP plus CHIPS for an Anthropic 1.6GW / $15bn Texas campus (WSJ, 2026-07-30), structurally the same move NVDA is making with neoclouds (see [[NBIS]]). Related: Anthropic renegotiated its Amazon agreement around TOKENS rather than COMPUTE HOURS, "a change that could raise Amazon's costs"** (The Information · Cathy Gao). **⑤ Google as the named cause of [[RDDT]]'s 23% fall on 07-31 — a ranking change down-weighting machine-translated content plus weak CTR conversion in AI Overviews, with GOOGL at ~40-50% of Reddit's inbound traffic, framed by Redburn as Search moving "towards a ZERO-CLICK WORLD."** ⚠️ Regulatory corollary logged alongside: **Jefferies flags US antitrust decisions beginning to bite [[AAPL]] Services partly through a LOWER REVENUE SHARE WITH GOOGL** — the same dominance that captures Reddit's traffic is what is under litigation. Cross-filed [[AMZN]], [[MSFT]], [[RDDT]], [[AAPL]], [[MEDIATEK]], [[ORCL]], [[ANTHROPIC]], [themes/custom-asic-tpu](themes/custom-asic-tpu.md), [themes/hyperscaler-capex](themes/hyperscaler-capex.md). |

| 2026-07-31 | ⭐ DELL | (wiki-ingest) — the neocloud demand that drives DELL's AI server book is now partly underwritten by NVDA itself, which is a change in the quality of that demand.** No rating or PT superseded. **The mechanism: NVDA is providing neoclouds with a MINIMUM REVENUE GUARANTEE on GPU capacity if they cannot lease it out, in exchange for a share of revenue above a threshold — with FIRMUS and SHARON AI named as early partners. GS reads this as broadening compute availability and "view[s] continued robust neocloud demand as positive for leading AI server vendors DELL and SMCI"** (Murphy/Ng, AI Project Pulse July 2026, 2026-07-31). ⚠️ **The second-order effect belongs on this page: the backstop transfers demand risk from the neocloud to the chip vendor, which sustains neocloud ORDERING even where end-demand softens. Near-term supportive for server volumes — but it means part of the AI server order book is underwritten by NVDA rather than by end users, and should not be read as independent end-demand.** Second item, on enterprise AI: GS argues **"expanding enterprise AI adoption should benefit enterprise-oriented hardware OEMs (DELL, HPE) and IT distributors (SNX, INGM),"** citing two named cases — **Starbucks building proprietary in-house AI tools for inventory and maintenance "with the intent to REPLACE third-party software from Microsoft, IBM, and Oracle," cutting ~$30mn from its enterprise-tech budget including ~$10mn of software spend; and Revolut pre-training PRAGMA on H1000 GPUs (65% more fraud cases caught, 2.3x better credit-default accuracy, 41% more relevant recommendations).** ⚠️ Note the Starbucks case is asymmetric — bullish for on-prem hardware, bearish for the enterprise software it displaces ([[MSFT]], [[ORCL]]). Cross-filed [[SMCI]], [[HPE]], [[NBIS]], [[NVDA]], [[MSFT]], [[ORCL]]. |
| 2026-07-31 | ⭐ CRWV | (wiki-ingest) — a new listed competitor coming at a valuation and revenue claim that would exceed CoreWeave's, plus a supplier-written demand collar that changes how the group should be underwritten.** No rating or PT superseded. **① Nscale (UK, NVDA-backed) is preparing an IPO with GS and JPM — investor day in New York the week of Aug 3, listing possibly this fall — at a floated ~$25bn vs $14.6bn in March. ⚠️ The claim that matters: Nscale projects 2027 revenue TRIPLING TO $30BN, "more than what analysts estimate CoreWeave will generate next year." The visible weakness in that number: its West Virginia flagship still needs "tens of billions" of financing AND HAS NO ANNOUNCED TENANT.** The playbook is explicitly CoreWeave's — **Nscale is buying Anyscale for $1.6bn, likened directly to CRWV's $1.7bn Weights & Biases purchase just before its March 2025 IPO.** Backers: >$6bn from 8090 Industries, Citadel, Jane Street; **Sheryl Sandberg and Nick Clegg** added to the board. ⚠️ Tape context against the timing: **CRWV −25% over the past month; Sharon AI −42% over the past month after +60% since its February IPO; Lambda delayed its listing; Crusoe talking to bankers** (The Information · Valida Pau, 2026-07-30). **② The structural item, and the most consequential for how this page reads competitor capacity: NVDA is writing GPU BACKSTOP PROGRAMS — a MINIMUM REVENUE GUARANTEE on GPU capacity a neocloud cannot lease out, in exchange for a share of revenue above a threshold, with FIRMUS and SHARON AI named as early partners** (GS · Murphy/Ng, AI Project Pulse July 2026, 2026-07-31). ⚠️ **Two consequences: (a) reported neocloud capacity and order flow are no longer clean reads on independent end-demand, because ordering can be sustained by the backstop rather than by lettings; and (b) it advantages backstopped competitors against a CoreWeave raising on its own credit, while concentrating the sector's credit chain on a single counterparty. The same pattern is appearing beyond NVDA — Google is reportedly providing a credit backstop PLUS CHIPS for an Anthropic 1.6GW / $15bn Texas campus (WSJ, 2026-07-30). Vendor-financed AI capacity is now a category, not a set of one-offs.** **③ CRWV was among the largest holdings in the force-sold Situational Awareness book (+22% on the unwind day, vs NBIS +27%, BE +27%, SNDK +26%). Fund detail: ~$20bn AUM, +439% in 1H26, up to 4x levered, then −67% in July; ~$16bn public book sold to Citadel in one block (MLP and Jane Street also bid); ~$5bn Anthropic stake retained.** ⚠️ Reading both ways: July's drawdown was substantially forced selling, **but CRWV −25% over the month shows the damage preceded and exceeded the bounce.** Cross-filed [[NBIS]], [[NVDA]], [[DELL]], [[SMCI]], [[ANTHROPIC]], [[GOOG]], [themes/ai-datacenter-power](themes/ai-datacenter-power.md). |
| 2026-07-31 | ⭐ COHR | (wiki-ingest) — direct supplier-side evidence on InP substrate scarcity, and Coherent is named in it.** No rating or PT superseded. **The datapoint: AXT's 2Q26 call disclosed a $25.4M long-term-agreement payment FROM COHERENT (plus $22.3M from Casela), booked as a liability and recognised as it ships — so it is NOT in AXT's reported quarterly revenue.** ⚠️ **Significant upfront cash to pre-fund a substrate vendor's capacity, rather than relying on spot allocation, is a scarcity price — and it is the most concrete evidence on this page that indium-phosphide supply is the binding constraint on the 800G→1.6T transition.** Supporting context from the same primary call: **AXT is ahead of schedule doubling InP capacity in 2026, the InP revenue opportunity is on track to MORE THAN TRIPLE by end-2026 with "continued significant expansion in 2027," backlog is >$100M, and "demand outpaces supply, no matter how fast they add capacity."** The demand mechanism maps directly onto Coherent's mix: **the 800G→1.6T transition with MORE LASERS PER TRANSCEIVER plus higher-spec 6-inch substrates, and hyperscalers "advancing toward NPO and CPO."** Also useful: **customers buying gallium arsenide are "also very interested in InP"** — VCSEL and InP demand are moving together, consistent with Coherent's dual exposure. AXT expects to be **"by far the largest indium phosphide producer in the world"** post-2027 expansion, with **China at ~40-60% of its InP revenue.** Backdrop: COHR named among JPM's beneficiaries of **~$750bn 2026 hyperscaler capex (+80% y/y)**, and sits in BofA's **second-wave** AI re-entry list of names still 30-50% off highs. **JPM COHR + LITE callback diarised 2026-08-14 10am ET; optical panel (Ranovus, Avicena) + 650 Group at the 08-12 HW & Semis forum.** Cross-filed [[AXTI]], [[LITE]], [[GLW]], [[POET]], [themes/optical-cpo](themes/optical-cpo.md). |
| 2026-07-31 | ⭐ AXTI | (wiki-ingest) — Q2 2026 PRINTED, and it was a blowout: record revenue, capacity ahead of schedule, and the prior bear case retired by Lumentum's own behaviour.** ⚠️ **THESIS DRIFT: the "into the print" section is superseded and retained as the pre-print bar, which was cleared. Superseded expectation: management's ~~"~$34m of Q2 revenue already realizable"~~ vs actual **InP $30.7m** (plus GaAs $6.6m, germanium $272k and $10.0m from consolidated raw-material JVs) — the InP line came in slightly under the realizable figure while total revenue was still the highest in company history.** Primary source: the **Q2 2026 earnings call**, notes taken live by @Gaetano ("Significant", 2026-07-30/31) — weighted because the author was a **public AXTI bear until late July**. ⚠️ **THE ACCOUNTING TRAP, logged prominently because it is the most likely misread on this page: the $22.3m from Coherent and $25.4m from Casela are booked as a LIABILITY and converted to revenue only as AXT ships against the agreements — "these didn't show up in the rev numbers this Q." Bridging LTA announcements to reported revenue double-counts.** **Net-new operating facts: AHEAD OF SCHEDULE on doubling InP capacity in 2026; InP revenue opportunity on track to MORE THAN TRIPLE by end-2026 with significant further expansion in 2027, driven partly by NEW CRYSTAL GROWTH FURNACE DESIGNS; backlog >$100M; ~$66M of Q3 revenue realizable TODAY without an export permit; ~$130M/QUARTER exiting 2027 (~$520M annualised at the low end); and an expectation of being "by far the largest indium phosphide producer in the world" post-expansion. Customer concentration is lower than the narrative implies — top 5 ~30% of revenue with NO customer above 10%.** ⚠️ **THE ITEM THAT RETIRES A BEAR ARGUMENT, in the author's own words: Lumentum had "felt confident in their long term supply agreement with SUMITOMO, covering 7 years… This was ONE OF THE BIGGEST REASONS WHY I WAS BEARISH ON AXT," and signed with AXT anyway months later — with the Lumentum CEO "open recently about how they need to MULTI SOURCE AGGRESSIVELY." The inference to carry: substrate COST is not the binding concern, AVAILABILITY is.** **China: InP revenue DOUBLED q/q, "not gated by permits… just straight capacity ramp + demand," at ~40-60% of InP revenue — which leaves substantial ex-China headroom, and the US permit unlock remains unforecast upside ("If that unlocks, you are looking at a major step up again that isn't being forecasted"). Some easing noted: "more regularity in the process of permits in certain geographic regions."** Demand mechanism stated three ways: **800G→1.6T as OVERLAPPING (not substituting) demand, MORE LASERS PER TRANSCEIVER, and higher-value larger-diameter substrates — plus hyperscalers "advancing toward NPO and CPO."** Logged as unguided, not a target: **management implied margins could exceed 50% with more volume and larger wafers but would not commit.** Also: **vertical integration into critical raw materials via subsidiaries, and GaAs/VCSEL customers "also very interested in InP"** — the two demand curves move together. **Tape: +13% intraday 07-30 then >25% MORE after hours, in a week where "just a couple days ago, amazing earnings were resulting in a 10-20% drawdown."** Per this page's convention (no `## Intra-quarter` table), everything was folded into `## Current state` + this record. | 2026-07-31 | ⭐ ASML | (wiki-ingest) — ASML named Bernstein's Best Idea for 3Q26, and the pricing variant got its sharpest formulation yet. No rating or PT superseded** (Bernstein Outperform / €2,500 unchanged; the €2,300→€2,500 raise was already logged at the 07-15 print). The **timing** is the substance: the Best Idea designation lands **two days after Bernstein ran its own emergency client call on the China-DUV competition scare (07-28) and after an ~8% drawdown**, so the house is stamping highest conviction into the dip rather than into strength. **Net-new and the most reusable number added: litho intensity "rising steadily from 20% to close to 30% as DRAM migrates to more EUV layers, and stays elevated above 30% for advanced logic"** — the cleanest quantification of litho intensity on this page, and it recasts ASML as a share-gainer *within* WFE rather than WFE beta. Also net-new: **High-NA modelled as adopted by "virtually all leading edge players in DRAM and logic by 2030"** — a standard, not an option. **The pricing leg is now decomposed by two independent sources and they agree consensus under-models it: Bernstein splits it into (i) ~20% EUV price increase over two years from the 3800E→F migration (mechanical, contracted) and (ii) potential LIKE-FOR-LIKE hikes (discretionary, the real variant). Chipstrat (Austin Lyons, 07-31) makes it falsifiable — "the way the street has 2027 modeled, ASML's customers end up paying LESS per wafer of capacity than they do today. That has never happened" — which means flat pricing is upside and hikes are large upside.** Management's own language shift is now on the page as evidence: **April "not the way we do business" → July "more flexibility for pricing."** `Sinal vs gestão` **Pricing power row upgraded to ✓ confirms** with the two-mechanism decomposition folded in, plus the guardrail that ASML measures value on a **narrower yardstick than [[TSM]]** (throughput/overlay vs end-product value) and that over-taking invites both competition and regulators — not hypothetical two days after the China scare. Corroborating cross-read filed: **BofA's ASM International note argues deposition/ALD intensity is rising off the same drivers (GAA, HBM, 4F2 DRAM, advanced packaging)** — intensity is inflecting across the litho *and* deposition franchises at once (Scemama, 2026-07-31). Positioning: buy-side re-entry into the AI trade is reported as **"limited to higher quality, lower-vol names, i.e. Nvidia, Hyperscalers, and Semicaps (Lam Research, Applied Materials, ASML)"** (BofA Fenske/Liu via Villani, 07-30/31). ⚠️ The Chipstrat piece is a paywalled preview — the 2027 revenue build, the price-per-capacity math and the three risks were NOT reproduced. Cross-filed [[TSM]], [[LRCX]], [[AMAT]], [[KLAC]], [themes/semicap-wfe](themes/semicap-wfe.md). |

| 2026-07-30 | ⭐ OPENAI | **Read-through from the MSFT 4QFY26 earnings call (held 2026-07-29 AMC; Satya Nadella / Amy Hood / Jonathan Neilson (IR)). 3 State-of-play bullets, 3 Full-log rows (07-29: capital/mixed, demanda/mixed, competicao/**bear**), 2 Signal-vs-management cells extended (Capital/Microsoft; Product/frontier — verdicts HELD, print reads APPENDED, prior verdicts preserved verbatim), intra-quarter window Jul 28 → Jul 29, 1 Sources link, this entry.** ⚠️ **Source-routing correction: the auto-router tagged this transcript "UBS" and dated it 2026-07-30 — both wrong. "UBS" came from the analyst roster (Karl Keirstead); it is Microsoft's own call, held 2026-07-29.** **PRIVATE NAME — no rating/PT/consensus to supersede, and nothing on this page is superseded.** **(1) THE OI&E DRAG — the item the page has been tracking is now a permanent reporting convention at Microsoft and is STILL unsized.** _"When adjusted for the impact of our investments in OpenAI, **other income and expense was $2.8 billion**, driven by the gain on investment in Anthropic"_; _"Earnings per share was $4.74, an increase of 23% **when adjusted for the impact from our investment in OpenAI**"_; Q1FY27 _"**Excluding any impact from our investments in OpenAI, other income and expense is expected to be roughly negative $100 million**."_ ⚠️ **The disclosure is deliberately asymmetric — Microsoft names and sizes the $3.2bn ANTHROPIC gain (a discrete item inside a $0.27 diluted-EPS benefit vs the April guide) and gives NO number for the OpenAI impact. Direction is certain (it is a drag: reported OI&E was materially below $2.8bn and reported EPS growth below +23%); MAGNITUDE IS UNDISCLOSED AND WAS DELIBERATELY NOT BACK-SOLVED.** Consequence for the page: the **ex-OpenAI OI&E baseline is now ≈ −$100M**, so equity-method losses land on an already-negative line, and MS's CY27-EPS-inflection thesis (losses roll off at the ~$13B cap; MS MSFT/OpenAI 2025-05-21) **got no update** — the cleanest public proxy for cumulative OpenAI losses stayed dark another quarter. Contrast logged: Microsoft's two frontier-lab positions moved in opposite directions in the same quarter and only one was quantified. **(2) THE CONCENTRATION NARRATIVE INVERTS, and Microsoft is now the party de-emphasising the relationship.** **Commercial bookings +18% ex-OpenAI vs +10% (+11% cc) including OpenAI Azure commitments; commercial RPO $678B +84% but +25% ex-OpenAI, with "ALL sequential commercial RPO growth… driven by commitments from customers outside of Frontier model companies"; WAD 2.3 yrs, ~30% next-12M (+37% y/y), beyond-12M portion +112%; FY26 cloud revenue >$214B "with nearly 90% from customers outside of Frontier model companies"; and an explicit pre-warning that "the significant OpenAI contract signed in the prior year will result in some quarterly volatility in both bookings and RPO growth rates."** Filed with the caveat that **this is contracted backlog, not OpenAI consumption** — no OpenAI-specific Azure revenue, no Azure AI split, and no update on whether OpenAI *inference* stays on Azure (UBS's 2025-09-02 swing factor, still open). Nothing on the restructured terms (IP to 2032 / rev-share from OpenAI to 2030 / AGI clause removed), the ~27% stake, or Musk v. OpenAI. **(3) THE COMPETITIVE READ, filed BEAR: Microsoft benchmarked its own model against GPT by name, on cost, inside its own flagship app — _"In Excel, MAI Code 1 Flash is delivering comparable quality to GPT56 [GPT-5.6] for the most common task while operating at significantly lower costs"_** — targeting the release Altman priced aggressively on 07-14. Plus MAI Code 1 Flash at **~10% lower median token usage** with higher acceptance rates in GitHub Copilot, MAI Cyber 1 Flash beating Anthropic's Mythos **at half the cost** on a **90%-cheap / 10%-frontier routing split**, **89%/84% GPU-cost reductions in D365/PowerPoint**, MAI Thinking One, an **11,000+ model catalog** and **multi-provider builders +5x YTD**. Strategic statement carried verbatim: _"the harness, context, memory, and action space are separate from any one model family… **every model is substitutable**"_ (justified on the Hugging Face incident and on _"the models are an **input**"_). **Read against UBS's 07-26 reflexivity note already on the page: the down-tiering UBS found INSIDE the OpenAI ladder is now also being run by Microsoft with MAI as the cheap tier — share loss, not just mix loss.** **Logged two-sided, not one-sided:** OpenAI stays in the catalog; **Maia 200 is "now supporting both OpenAI and MAI models"** (first disclosure of OpenAI workloads on Microsoft's accelerator, ~30% better perf/$ vs latest fleet hardware); Cobalt VMs run OpenAI workloads; Levi Strauss runs OpenAI **and** Anthropic on Foundry across 1,000+ agents; and Microsoft's web-grounding layer for agents is **"already being used by the most popular AI assistants, including ChatGPT"** — ChatGPT as a Microsoft customer. ⚠️ **DeepSeek V4 was not mentioned on the call at all** (cf. Stratechery 06-23) — MAI, not a Chinese open-weight model, is the cost defence Microsoft presented. MSFT claims are logged as vendor claims (own benchmarks, "most common task" scoping, system-vs-model comparison in the Cyber case). Cross-filed [[MSFT]], [[ANTHROPIC]]. |

| 2026-07-30 | ⭐ NVDA | SemiAnalysis "The Wild Wild West Of LEGO Datacenters" (2026-07-29). 1 intra-quarter row (07-29, produto/bull) + 1 Sources entry. NOTHING SUPERSEDED — no rating, PT, house estimate or existing NVDA number changed; purely additive.** ⚠️ **Logged as a READ-THROUGH: the note's subject is the modular-datacenter vendor landscape (Vertiv/Flex/nVent/Quanta/Eaton/Schneider et al.), not NVDA.** Routed here because the note documents something the page did not carry at all — **NVDA's reference design has moved from the rack to the building.** What it adds: **DSX**, the reference design for the entire AI factory, dated — Omniverse digital-twin blueprint unveiled at **GTC Washington Oct-2025**, **formalized as the Vera Rubin DSX reference design in March 2026**, since expanded into the full DSX platform, covering compute/networking/storage/hardware-cluster design **plus the facilities side: power, cooling, controls, even civil, structural and architectural design.** The two named products, verbatim: **"Nvidia's DSX Max-Q maximizes token per watt within a fixed power budget, and DSX Flex facilitates the connection [of] the facility to grid services, dynamically adjusting power draw and orchestrating demand with hybrid onsite generation"** — DSX Flex pulls demand-response/BTM orchestration into the reference architecture itself. Deployment workflow via the Omniverse DSX Blueprint (digital twin → simulate layouts, power topologies, thermal behaviour, operational policies → optimize pre-construction → reuse across sites), with **CoreWeave already using DSX Air**. The adoption evidence is the point: Cadence, Dassault Systèmes, Eaton, Jacobs, Nscale, Phaidra, Procore, PTC, Schneider Electric, Siemens, Switch, Trane, Vertiv — **Vertiv's OneCore became the building block of the Vera Rubin DSX reference design as of March 2026** (12.5 MW pods), **Schneider works with NVIDIA on 30+ reference designs** (EcoStruxure Pod, up to 40 racks), **Siemens is among the first suppliers with both IEC and UL variants for Rubin DSX**, **Jacobs is one of only two engineering firms working with the DSX reference architecture (the other Bechtel)**, Hut 8's Beacon Point is designed around DSX, and Foxconn unveiled its Modular Data Center concept at GTC 2026. **Read: moat-widening beyond CUDA — it standardizes the building around NVDA's roadmap, so the switching cost of a competing accelerator now includes the facility. Flip side logged, not smoothed: a reference architecture reaching into civil/structural design also transfers more schedule risk onto NVDA's spec stability.** This also sharpens the PhotonCap caveat logged the same day from the Lumentum meeting ("it rides on one company's architecture choice") — that concentration risk is now documented on the power/cooling/EPC side as well as the optical side. Cross-filed [[VRT]], [[ETN]], [[FLEX]], [[PWR]], [[NVT]], [[CRWV]], [themes/ai-datacenter-power.md](themes/ai-datacenter-power.md), [themes/800v-dc-power.md](themes/800v-dc-power.md). |
| 2026-07-30 | ⭐ NVDA | Aurelion Research / PhotonCap "A Conversation with Lumentum" (2026-07-29). 1 intra-quarter row (07-29, produto/mixed) + 1 Sources entry. NOTHING SUPERSEDED — no rating, PT, house estimate or existing NVDA number changed.** ⚠️ **Logged as a READ-THROUGH: the subject company is Lumentum, and the source is a joint independent publication (management meeting + investment analysis by Aurelion Research, technical comments by PhotonCap), not a bank; interview answers are the authors' paraphrase, not verbatim management statements.** **What it adds that this page did not have. (1) A supplier-side explanation of NVDA's CPO motive, from Lumentum management: CPO today is "opportunistic rather than forced. Nvidia deploys it because a faster, more power efficient laser frees up power that can be redirected to the GPUs, which buys system performance. Copper has not run out of headroom yet" — with copper hitting the wall "late 2027," when the first scale-up CPO deployments or MPO connectors arrive, driven by rack-to-rack links beyond ~3m. That CONFIRMS the earlier end of the CPO-timing range and sits against MS's 07-28 markdown of 2028 CPO *scale* and the unresolved Fubon post-Kyber rack decision already on this page; not reconciled. (2) An NVDA-sourced power number quoted by PhotonCap — "Nvidia's own material puts CPO at 3.5x lower power than pluggable transceivers" — with the mechanism stated as a fixed power envelope ("every watt saved on networking is a watt handed to the GPUs") and the conclusion "copper is not forcing this wave; the power budget is." ⚠️ PhotonCap's caveat retained verbatim because it is the systemic risk for the whole optical complex: "the weak spot is not the voluntariness but that it rides on one company's architecture choice." (3) Two Rubin/domain mechanics net-new here: Rubin "relies on bi-directional signaling to reach its bandwidth targets, and bi-directional signaling over copper degrades substantially at 448G per lane," with an unknown time-to-market for 448G copper; and NVDA "has begun moving from 72 GPU domains toward as many as 576 by 2027, and copper is already confined on an NV72 rack" — so once a domain spans racks, "linking must be optical regardless of copper's current efficiency." (4) Second-sourcing posture: "NVIDIA's technical blog names three ELS vendors, Lumentum, Sumitomo, and Coherent," and "the same day Lumentum got its $2B, Coherent got an identical $2B" — NVDA is not single-sourcing its external light source. Jensen's copper-for-scale-up / photonics-for-scale-out framing and NVDA's stated need for copper capacity both logged.** Cross-filed [LITE.md](LITE.md), [COHR.md](COHR.md), [themes/optical-cpo.md](themes/optical-cpo.md). |

NOTHING SUPERSEDED — but three tensions are now on the page and are deliberately left unresolved.** **Why it ranks: this is management (Kathryn Ta, VP Investor Relations), which outranks the brokers and experts that make up most of the quarter's flow. ⚠️ Provenance caveat carried on the page: the authors state interview responses are paraphrased from their notes and "do not represent verbatim statements by Lumentum or its management."** **CONFIRMED from management:** the **">30% greater demand than is physically suppliable"** imbalance (already on the page from Q3FY26); **all EML capacity locked in LTAs through FY27** with non-signers risking their whole allocation; **five fabs** (consistent with the Greensboro 5th-fab line already on the page); **components ~65% / systems ~35%**; **first scale-up CPO deployments at "late 2027"** — which confirms mgmt's existing late-cal-'27 mark and, critically, **contradicts LITE's own Director of Silicon Photonics R&D from 07-23 (CPO trial ~2028, hyperscaler deployment ~2030, GPU-GPU copper for at least one more generation) and sits against MS's 07-28 markdown of 2028 CPO *scale*. Contradiction #1, left on the page and flagged in the Sinal table — a management confirmation on timing is stronger evidence than an expert's, and the divergence inside one company is the alpha.** **CONTRADICTS / COMPLICATES the scarcity trade, from management's own mouth: Japanese EML capacity is up EIGHT-FOLD in two and a half years (two Japan fabs make most of the world's EML supply), a new fab is ramping but "not yet producing wafers," tools have been added at every site and several building shells expanded — and Aurelion flags it itself: "Eight-fold EML capacity in two and a half years cuts against a pure scarcity thesis. Lumentum is racing to relieve the bottleneck that gives it pricing power." PhotonCap converts it into the sharper claim: "wafer starts and qualified yield at the newest spec are different numbers… the binding constraint is yield at the highest power bins, not floor space… the eight fold number overstates the relief, and… the shortage premium itself is not durable while the margin that survives lives in yield and mix, not in the shortage price." Net: the thing you underwrite shifts from shortage PRICE to yield/mix DURABILITY. No estimate on this page changes; the Sinal-vs-management "Demand vs supply" row moved from a clean ✓ to ✓ + ⚠ (old value: "✓ confirms (supply is the constraint)" — retained in the cell).** **Contradiction #2, left open: Aurelion's bear #1 states "Lumentum still runs 4 inch wafers and is trying to catch up" against Coherent's 6-inch line (~2.25x usable area per wafer, more die at lower cost, COHR InP sold out through 2027) — while the 07-23 expert call on this page confirmed LITE's purchase of a 6-inch III-V fab in North Carolina. Current volume production 4-inch, Greensboro 6-inch; both stated, not reconciled.** **Contradiction #3, left open: two customers at 26% and 12% of fiscal Q3 2026 revenue (added to Risks) vs Citi's 07-16 mgmt callback that NVDA is still not a top-10 / 10%+ customer (low-single-digit %) — so the 26% customer is probably not NVDA. The FY25 10-K split (Customer A 16.0% / Customer B 15.4%) is NOT superseded; it stands as the annual figure, with the quarterly 26%/12% added as the more current concentration read.** **NET-NEW, additive:** PhotonCap's **third light-source platform** — GaAs VCSEL alongside InP EML and InP high-power CW, with **>10 billion emitters shipped** for smartphone 3D sensing and an **OFC 2026 1060nm VCSEL + photodetector 2D monolithic array co-packaged with a host ASIC targeting UCIe/PCIe slow-and-wide scale-up, >150°C, channel sparing** ("what sits on the roadmap is not VCSEL technology. It is commercial adoption in AI chip-to-chip links") — a materially stronger architecture hedge than the page carried, and directly relevant to the LightCounting InP-91%→79% / VCSEL-9%→21% lane-share debate; the **CPO-delay-extends-the-EML-cycle** frame (an integration-driven delay pushes CW/ELSFP right while extending EML, so cannibalization "flips into a cushion"); **Global Semi Research channel numbers** (NVDA CW guidance to LITE+COHR ~40MM units in January → ~100MM by April/May, back-solving to ~200-300k CPO switches in 2027 and 600-800k in 2028, aligned to TSMC COUPE — **channel, not company disclosure, per Aurelion's own weighting**); the specific **SemiAnalysis 2026-06-09 mechanism** behind the June selloff (scale-out CPO revised down through FY27, scale-up pushed to FY29, on a single very limited ASIC with difficult integration economics); the **McKinsey 800G shortfall** (~half of demand in FY27, extending through 2029); **a second, ground-up NVDA-funded US fab** distinct from the Greensboro conversion (the page previously carried only a "phase-four fab" watch-item); **Greensboro revenue contribution ~6 quarters from the May call = "late 2027 at the earliest,"** slightly earlier than the page's Greensboro-2028 mark (both retained); **EML production expanding ~40% annually** (lower than the page's mgmt-sourced ">50% EML unit growth by Dec-Q 2026" — both retained, neither superseded); **~60% of the EML market** (a different basis from the ~50% AI EML *unit* share from Citi/Mizuho — explicitly NOT treated as superseding it); PhotonCap's **RIN/COD/SMSR/FIT moat definition** ("qualified usable power at operating temperature, multiplied by spectral quality, reliability, and high volume yield"; "headline specs are what challengers close first… distribution tails, noise, and lifetime statistics close last"); the **EML-vs-CW product map** ("EML is today's cash flow and high power CW is the next mix shift"); **NVIDIA's technical blog naming three ELS vendors — Lumentum, Sumitomo, Coherent** ("second sourcing is in progress, not a hypothesis"), with **COHR's OFC 2026 6-inch InP ramp + 400mW CW** and **AAOI's 400mW narrow-linewidth sampling (Dec 2025)** landing outside the FY27 LTA window; and a **Zetta Semiconductor update** (mass-producible 100G PAM4 EML at OECC 2025, 200G/lane at ACP Nov 2025 — "device level performance deserves to be taken seriously now"), which extends the DSBJ/China-EML watch-item already in Risks. **NEW RATING/PT MARKS (none superseded — none were previously on the page): TD Cowen cut its PT to $800 from just over $990** — the first PT *reduction* logged here, from the page's cleanest structural bear (Hold since 2026-03-11, no PT previously recorded); **UBS re-affirmed a hold on LITE** (prior UBS content here was Sunny Lin's Asia CPO work, not a LITE rating); **Citi reiterated Buy $1,100 with a catalyst watch (confirms 07-16)**; the full sell-side range is now **$900 to $1,400**, widening the page's previously recorded ~$1,100-$1,270 cluster in both directions; stock **~20% below its all-time high**, with Aurelion's framing that "most of the photonics supercycle has been realized and played out." The Sinal table's **Valuation** row keeps its original "⚠ nuance (multiple already prices it in)" and adds the dispersion read. **Token-economics exchange cross-filed to [themes/tokenmaxxing.md](themes/tokenmaxxing.md); CPO/copper/laser-supply content to [themes/optical-cpo.md](themes/optical-cpo.md); peer/competitive content to [COHR.md](COHR.md); read-throughs to [NVDA.md](NVDA.md), [AVGO.md](AVGO.md), [AMD.md](AMD.md).** LITE reports **8/11 AMC**. |

| 2026-07-28 | ⭐ SKHYNIX | **/wiki-ingest · Q2 2026 PRINT (2026-07-28) — new-print reset.** New `## Current state` for the June quarter (the Q1 2026 block plus all the standing capacity/HBM4/MR-MUF/packaging deep-dive material is retained inline below it); archived the Q1 2026 intra-quarter window (Apr 23 → Jul 28, **130 lines** — the largest window on the wiki) into this Changelog; opened a fresh Q2 2026 window; rolled the management-evolution table forward (Q2 2025 drops off, **Q2 2026 added**). **Superseded per the thesis-drift rule** — prior values preserved: *Current state* previously led with **Q1 2026 revenue ₩52.6tn (+60% q/q, +198% y/y), operating profit ₩37.6tn, OP margin 72%, net profit ₩40.3tn, EBITDA ₩41.3tn, net cash ₩35tn.** **What printed: revenue ₩79.3tn vs ~₩83.9tn expected and operating profit ₩60.54tn vs ₩64.2tn consensus — the first headline miss of the cycle.** ⚠️ **The qualifier matters as much as the miss: local Korean brokers had already cut OP to ~₩60tn and Jefferies' Jeff Kim was at ₩62tn**, so the print is in line with the most recent numbers and only misses the stale screen consensus. Margin structure intact — **GM 83%, OM 76%, EBITDA margin 81%** — and the *level* still rose sequentially off Q1. **The margin trajectory this page has tracked (41% → 47% → 58% → 72%) therefore breaks its ascent at 76%.** **Cause of the shortfall, per Jefferies: the HBM4 ramp is late** — HBM4 shipments did begin in Q2 at customer-required speeds and **1cnm HBM4E samples went to a major customer in 1H**, but the ramp slipped relative to plan. Other management detail logged: **DRAM Q2 bits +HSD with ASP +30% q/q, 2026 bit growth guided mid-20s%, Q3 bits +10% server-focused**; **SOCAMM2 fully commercialised on 1C**; demand **broadening as AI evolves into agentic form** across HBM/server DRAM/eSSD; **PC and smartphone softness explicitly attributed to difficulty securing memory volumes, not weak end-demand.** **Two developments change the shape of the debate rather than the numbers.** **(1) The [[NVDA]] anchor is being re-read as a pricing constraint.** Jefferies expects the miss to fuel the view that **"Hynix has been less aggressive than [[SAMSUNG]] on pricing given its commitments to NVDA on HBM"** — i.e. the supplier-differentiation moat this page underwrites may also be a price ceiling. **(2) Capex is stepping up ~80% to the high-₩40tn range and is being pulled in, with no buyback despite large net cash** (Culpium; Jefferies) — landing in the same week Jefferies research published **"HAS MEMORY PEAKED?": price hikes tracking 15-20% vs 25-30% expected, memory prices peaking ~1H27, and 2027 consensus requiring ~US$2tn of US AI capex to be achievable**, with **HBM4 costs jumping to US$31-32/GB**. That is the bear case in one line — peak-margin cash committed to capacity into a forecast price peak. It is also consistent with management's own long-standing argument that ~70% OP margins are "not high enough" to fund the greenfield/hybrid-bonding step-up. **Tape context (not a company event, but it dominated the reaction):** on 2026-07-27 the **KOSPI fell 10.7% with a sell sidecar and the year's eighth circuit breaker, Samsung -12%, SK Hynix -13%**, driven by **CXMT's IPO debut at +466% — China's second-largest IPO ever — "making the memory threat feel suddenly very real"** plus a live-Fed-hike scare (~40% odds priced). On the print the stock opened **-6% pre-market and recovered to -2.3%**, while **[[MU]] fell a further ~4% after hours on top of -8.9% intraday**. Also logged: an unverified @zerohedge claim that SK Hynix slumped below a US IPO price — **flagged, not adopted**, as it is uncorroborated elsewhere in this ingest; and a Korean-press exclusive (@jukan05) that **LPDDR6 mass production starts in 2H with Xiaomi as first customer**, which sits in tension with the same-day comment that PC/mobile volumes are being rationed. Cross-filed [[MU]], [[SAMSUNG]], [[NVDA]], [themes/hbm-memory](themes/hbm-memory.md), [themes/china-export](themes/china-export.md). |

| 2026-07-28 | ⭐ NVDA | (/run-inbox — the Fubon PRIMARY PDF + 2 MS secondaries)** — **3 intra-quarter rows, 1 existing row annotated, Product/Rubin Signal cell extended, 3 Sources links. NO rating, PT or house estimate superseded** (MS Top Pick $288, BofA Buy $350, Barclays OW $275, DB Hold $255, UBS house $280-285, Arete Buy $283 all stand; Fubon's note carries no NVDA rating). **The single most valuable thing this run does is RESOLVE the contradiction this page flagged and deliberately left open earlier today.** The 07-28 `/wiki-ingest` entry below recorded that *"the two Jefferies relays state the 2028 SoIC revision in opposite directions (Wong: to 90k from 70k; Beavington: to 70k from 90k)"* and left it unreconciled. **The primary Fubon PDF settles it verbatim: "Compared to our earlier forecast of 90k capacity in 4Q28, we revise down our capacity expansion forecast to 70k in 4Q28." So 70k is the live mark, 90k the superseded one — Beavington was right, the Wong relay inverted.** The cause is stated too: **[[TSM]] is slowing SoIC expansion to prioritise CoWoS**, which is also why Fubon flags **potential upside to its 220kwpm end-2028 CoWoS number** (end-2027 held at 180kwpm). **A SECOND relay error corrected on the same row: the interposer counts are inverted. Fubon says "12 and 10 interposers per CoWoS wafer for Rubin and Rubin Ultra, respectively" — Rubin 12, Rubin Ultra 10, not the reverse.** The conclusion is unchanged (Rubin Ultra's interposer only marginally larger post-cancellation → chip output 12.4mn 2027 vs 8.2mn 2026), but the numbers on the page were backwards and are now annotated in place rather than deleted. **Net-new #1, and it needs its own guardrail: NVDA's TOTAL capacity allocation is 1.03mn wafers in 2027 vs 629k in 2026 — this is TSMC + SPIL and is BROADER than the TSMC-CoWoS-only line of 945,000 wafers (50.0% of 1.89mn; 586,500 / 50.1% in 2026; 346,800 / 51.4% in 2025). The ~85k gap is SPIL capacity supporting Vera CPU. Do not net the two.** **Net-new #2, a relative-advantage datapoint the page did not have: HBM4 costs NVDA US$31-32/GB (from US$17-18/GB HBM3e in 2026), while "other GPU and custom-ASIC projects… may reach US$35-36 per GB, meaning the HBM cost will double in 2027." HBM inflation is real for NVDA (~+80%) but it buys ~10-15% cheaper than the merchant/ASIC field — a scale edge that partly offsets the ASIC-TCO bear this page carries from JPM/UBS.** On that BOM Fubon models the **Rubin ASP at US$78-80K on a 75-80% chip GM** (confirmed against the Beavington relay, not re-entered). **Net-new #3 — the fullest account on this page of what replaces Kyber, folded into the Product/Rubin Signal cell: Kyber was cancelled/delayed in MARCH/APRIL on mid-plane bandwidth issues; it carried 576 compute dies per rack (144 GPUs × 4 compute dies); checks say NVDA still targets 576 in the replacement, via either (a) four Oberon racks per system on traditional scale-out (144 compute dies each) or (b) a two-rack "Taycan" design at 144 GPUs × 2 compute dies per rack with NPO/CPO for the inter-rack scale-out and cable retained for intra-rack scale-up. Fubon explicitly declines to name winners and losers because neither is finalised — but says "nVidia is still aggressive in the NPO/CPO solution despite a lot of market noise."** Positioning kept in Fubon's own words on both sides: short-term the **NVDA supply chain "could potentially underperform due to Rubin's lower-than-expected volume in 2H26 and uncertain design change in the next generation rack design,"** with Fubon preferring the CPU/SPE/TPU chains — against **"token cost is still a more important factor in driving CSP's capex, and we remain positive about AI market demand."** **Two secondary sources added as separate rows. (1) MS "The Paths to 25-50% GenAI ROIC" (Nowak/Byrd/Wood, 07-27) — logged explicitly as a demand-durability read-through and NOT as an NVDA mark (the note has no NVDA rating or PT): MS standardises all three GenAI unit-economics frameworks on 1GW of NVDA GB300 (410,256 chips/GW, US$39bn all-in capex/GW = US$23bn IT on 5yr + US$16bn non-IT on 15yr, ~US$4mn/GB300 rack, 75% utilisation, US$8.50/hr, 2,750 tokens/sec/GPU) and gets ~31% ROIC / ~67% incremental EBIT margin on hyperscaler GPU rental, ~46% / ~75% on a model-enabled API on owned infra, ~25% / ~31% on 3P infra. The buy-side point is that the returns math on the CURRENT generation clears a normal hurdle at every layer — the most direct available answer to the Crisafulli/Chanos/Burry "hyperscalers can't generate FCF" bear.
