The 7.3% / ~138k / top-pick-on-the-pullback material is CONFIRMED verbatim against the primary and was NOT re-entered — only net-new content was added and the section was marked as confirmed.** **Net-new from the primary: the full series rather than the single 2027 point — MediaTek at 0.0% (2023-25) → 1.5% (2026) → 7.3% (2027) of TSMC CoWoS = 0 → 17,400 → 137,700 wafers. The 2026 base is the useful part: 17,400 wafers is effectively a standing start, so 2027 is a ~8x step, and it moves MediaTek to the fourth-largest TSMC CoWoS customer (behind NVDA 945,000, AVGO 421,800, AWS 196,500; ahead of AMD's 132,300).** Also net-new: **Fubon's causal ordering, verbatim — "MediaTek is able to get more capacity allocation in 2027, and therefore, we think it is possible that MediaTek could further raise its custom-ASIC market share in 2027." Allocation is the cause, share the consequence, which is the framing this page has been arguing (supply, not demand, is the gate on the 2027 guide).** ⚠️ **Basis guardrail added: 137,700 is TSMC-only CoWoS; Fubon's "total allocation" figures for other names in the same note are on a different basis (NVDA 1.03mn = TSMC + SPIL; AMD 300k = TSMC + ASE) and no total-allocation figure is given for MediaTek — so 137,700 must not be compared against those.** **⚠️ THESIS DRIFT LOGGED, and it is the material item of this run — Fubon has moved against its own prior position on the v9 packaging path.** **Superseded position, retained here in full: Fubon (2026-07-08) reported that Google initially planned Intel EMIB-T for TPU V9 in late-2027/2028 but asked Intel to lift EMIB-T yield to 95%+ before production, judged that "very challenging to achieve by mid-2027," and concluded Google would "at last rely on TSMC's [2028 CoWoS] expansion plan." Fubon (2026-06-05) had similarly held that "Intel EMIB-T is not yet MP-ready, so the V9 priority stays TSMC CoWoS-L" on a 60-70% EMIB yield vs CoWoS 95%+.** **New position (2026-07-28), verbatim: "Based on our checks, Google plans to have 12-15mn TPUs in 2028. Entering 2028, Google's TPUs will enter the V9 generation with four compute dies, suggesting that their capacity consumption will more than double in 2028 vs. 2027. Although we do not have the detailed allocation yet, we think it is difficult to reach Google's target with TSMC alone, and INTEL'S SUPPLY IS A MUST BY 2028." Plus: "although there is still a lot of noise on Intel's EMIB-T progress, especially its learning curve improvement, we see Intel becoming more positive in capacity expansion, which is positive to the Google supply chain" — with EMIB capacity at 10-12k/month by end-2026 and a plan to double to 24-25k/month by end-2027, and Intel's CFO earmarking a large portion of the announced capex increase for advanced packaging such as EMIB-T.** **What changed, precisely, and it is not a yield capitulation: the 07-08 argument was that the TSMC fallback would absorb Google's demand; the 07-28 argument is that the demand is too large for TSMC to absorb, so Intel is required regardless of where the yield debate lands. The learning-curve caveat is explicitly retained — it is reclassified from "reason the ramp doesn't happen" to "risk to the ramp."** **Why it matters on this page: it removes the last major house arguing MediaTek's v9 has a clean TSMC CoWoS escape hatch — which is item (2) on MS's list of questions for the 7/31 call ("Intel EMIB-T production yield and whether there is a TSMC CoWoS backup plan"). The source of the backup-plan thesis has now answered it in the negative, on capacity rather than yield grounds. NO RATING OR PT SUPERSEDED — MS reiterates Overweight, PT $400** (Nowak's 07-23 post-print mark), and Barclays $405, BofA $430, DB $430, Citi $447, GS $450 and JPM $460 all stand. **⚠️ ONE THESIS DRIFT, AND IT IS A HOUSE REVERSING ITSELF: Fubon's 2026-07-08 conclusion that Google would "ultimately rely on TSMC's 2028 CoWoS expansion" for TPU V9 is SUPERSEDED by Fubon's own 2026-07-28 note — "it is difficult to reach Google's target with TSMC alone, and Intel's supply is a must by 2028."** The 07-08 text is **retained verbatim in the TPU/merchant-silicon section as the dated prior mark**, annotated as superseded, and recorded here for the record. **The reversal is driven by demand, not by an improved yield view, and that is the part to carry: "Google plans to have 12-15mn TPUs in 2028. Entering 2028, Google's TPUs will enter the V9 generation with FOUR COMPUTE DIES, suggesting that their capacity consumption will MORE THAN DOUBLE in 2028 vs. 2027"** — a four-die package does not fit inside TSMC's book at that volume, regardless of what EMIB-T yields do. Fubon concedes the yield doubt it raised on 07-08 is unanswered ("still a lot of noise on Intel's EMIB-T progress, especially its learning curve improvement") but is now constructive on Intel *capacity*: **EMIB 10-12k/month by end-2026 doubling to 24-25k/month by end-2027**, CFO earmarking a great portion of new capex for advanced packaging; Ibiden's next call named as the tell. TSMC baseline added: **CoWoS 180kwpm end-2027 → 220kwpm end-2028**, with SoIC 4Q28 cut from 90k to 70k to prioritise CoWoS, and ASE FoCoS raised to 50kwpm. **Effect on the page's standing three-way v9 disagreement: it narrows to 2-vs-1 for EMIB-T** — Fubon crosses to join **UBS Asia (07-27)**, leaving **JPM · Gokul (07-08, "stays on CoWoS," Qualcomm/MediaTek-packaged)** as the holdout, while **UBS's own Arcuri (SemiBytes, 07-27) still calls EMIB-T "very low yields and immaturity."** **No view adopted; the divergence is narrower, not resolved.** ⚠️ **Second flag, deliberately left open: a basis conflict on the same 12-15mn number from the same house.** Jefferies · Sherman Chang relayed it on 07-17 (post-TSMC-2Q call with Fubon) as a **chip/die-basis** figure — "based on chips" — and Fubon now restates it as "12-15mn TPUs" against a V9 carrying **four compute dies per package**. Those cannot both be right at face value (12-15mn dies ≈ 3-4mn packages; 12-15mn packages ≈ 48-60mn dies). Both readings kept verbatim; for calibration UBS's 07-27 EMIB-T model has **2.3mn Google chipsets in 2028** at four XPUs per package, which is consistent with the package count being low-single-digit millions. **Third, and the sharpest cross-check against this page's own equity narrative: Fubon calls Google's 2028 target "very aggressive" EXPLICITLY "despite market concerns on Google capex and negative FCF"** — i.e. the packaging supply chain is being told to build for a plan the equity market punished with a -7.8% week on the first negative-FCF quarter. That is the supply-side/equity-side divergence stated by a source that has no equity axe on GOOGL (its top TPU pick is MediaTek). **On the MS note: the ROIC framework is the material addition, not the PT.** All three models standardise on **1GW of GB300 (410,256 chips, $39bn all-in capex/GW, ~$6bn/yr depreciation, 75% utilisation, $7.6bn/GW opex, 21% tax)**, and **GOOGL is the only name MS puts on both sides** — bullish "hyperscalers AMZN, GOOGL, MSFT" *and* "model developers GOOGL and META." GCP leg (**~67% incremental EBIT margin, ~31% ROIC** at $8.50/hr) plus Gemini leg (**~75% margin, ~46% ROIC** on owned infra at 2,750 tok/s/GPU and $1.75/mn) is the first published statement that Alphabet captures both the landlord and the tenant spread on the same gigawatt — the quantified form of the vertical-integration argument Google's own Principal Architect made on 06-23. The rented-infra framework (**~25% NOPAT margin** at $7.75/hr) prices the ~$50/W SpaceX arrangement as a bridge cost. **Two caveats logged rather than buried:** MS states reported near-term incremental margins run **below** the band (prior-gen mix, traditional-cloud mix, unmodelled opex), so this does not contradict the 35.6% reported Cloud OI margin; and MS explicitly notes the frameworks are **GB300-based** — "while custom ASICs and prior GPU generation have lower costs… we believe chip pricing/hour and token throughput are also lower" — so **these are not TPU economics and should not be quoted as such**. Risk-reward detail added (all net-new except the $400): bull **$450**, bear **$225**, the **~1.6x PEG / ~35% peer-median premium** framing, **spot $319.74**, mean consensus PT **$420.48** (range $220-$515), **89% OW / 11% EW**, options-implied **P(>$400) ~23.5% vs P(<$225) ~15.3%** — **the only one of MS's four internet names where the base case is more likely than the bear**, which reads the 07-22 drawdown as capex de-rating rather than thesis impairment. Estimates: total GAAP operating income **129,039 → 176,395 → 227,461 → 280,882** (+36.7%/+29.0%/+23.5%, the sharpest contrast on the complex with META's +3.0% 2028 OI growth on a comparable capex build); MS vs cons FY26e revenue **500,611 vs 495,765**, EBITDA **241,667 vs 232,729**, net income **255,100 vs 234,369**, EPS **20.62 vs 19.37**. Two anomalies logged, not corrected: MS shows **two operating-income series** in the same table (129,039→280,882 and 137,054→287,432), and its FY26e **net income exceeds its own EBITDA** (255,100 vs 241,667) — plausibly the Anthropic revaluation BofA models (07-16), but MS does not label it. House cross-check: MS's **$15 '27 EPS sits below the Capstone model's $16.20** (2026-06-05), so the house stays above MS on the out-year. That's been about FIVE OR SIX QUARTERS IN A ROW."** This is the cleanest answer on the page to the third leg of JPM's 07-27 "worst EDA sentiment I've seen" survey — *"the magnitude of the beats has been shrinking."* Both can be true: the frequency is unbroken, the magnitude compressed, which relocates the problem to the **bar** rather than to delivery. **(2) AGENTIC EDA UPGRADED FROM "ADDITIVE GROWTH VECTOR" (the note's language) TO "a DEMAND ACCELERATOR for these guys."** That is a claim about mechanism — agents pull *more* volume through the existing engines — and it is the sell-side stating what had previously only been management's own anti-cannibalisation argument (Devgan: "all these AI tools are increasing the usage of our tools"). The AI/agentic Sinal row was already upgraded ⚠ → ✓ earlier today; the clause was added and **the verdict left unchanged**, since this strengthens an existing conclusion rather than moving one. **(3) "They've got the FULL DESIGN FLOW NOW OPEN AS AN OFFERING"** — a completeness claim not in the note, and the thing that makes CFO Wall's three-tier monetisation (subscription anchor + usage-based + "virtual engineer") sellable as a single product rather than as point tools. ⚠️ **Process note for the run: this route was MISSED by the keyword router and is genuine primary content, not a read-through — the webcast's EDA segment is a standalone Cadence discussion ("Sean did mention EDA, and we had the results for Cadence last night… I thought it was very strong").** Same webcast cross-filed to [[ARM]], [[AAPL]], [[STX]], [[WDC]], [[GLW]], [[ANET]]. |

New value: 22.3% / 421,800 wafers,** with the full Figure-5 series now **16.2% (2025) → 22.5% (2026) → 22.3% (2027) = 109,650 → 263,100 → 421,800 wafers.** The Sources entry for the 06-05 note is annotated so the stale figure is not re-read as live. **The revision has to be held in two pieces because they point opposite ways, and the page was previously only carrying one of them.** **Piece one, positive and under-weighted here until now: on a CoWoS pool going 675k → 1,170k → 1,890k wafers, AVGO's absolute TSMC CoWoS wafers roughly 2.4x in 2026 and rise another ~60% in 2027. In wafer terms AVGO is the single largest gainer after NVDA. The share line flattening near 22% is a denominator effect, not a volume problem — and this page's existing framing ("2027 CoWoS share revised down") reads more bearish than the underlying numbers support.** **Piece two, the genuine negative: the reallocated share goes precisely to the two names AVGO competes with. Fubon states the mechanism explicitly — "we see that MediaTek is able to get more capacity allocation in 2027, and therefore, we think it is possible that MediaTek could further raise its custom-ASIC market share in 2027" — and names MediaTek its TOP PICK FOR TPU, calling the recent pullback a re-entry opportunity. That is the sharpest available statement of the head-to-head from a house covering both sides.** **⚠️ Two guardrails recorded so the cut is not over-read: (i) this is TSMC-ONLY CoWoS and a broker capacity-model assumption, not a TSMC or AVGO disclosure — Fubon's "total allocation" figures elsewhere in the same note (NVDA 1.03mn, AMD 300k) include SPIL/ASE and are a different basis; (ii) it is a UNIT/wafer claim and carries no revenue-share implication. MS (Joe Moore 07-14, Charlie Chan 07-26) still models AVGO at ~80% of TPU REVENUE on higher ASP and gross margin while conceding MediaTek closes the unit gap — the two views are compatible and both remain on the page.** **Offsetting positive in the same note: ASE's FoCoS capacity raised to 50kwpm for 2027 "to support CPU and networking customers' demand," superseding Fubon's own 45k (06-05, itself raised from 30k) — that is the platform carrying AVGO's switch chipsets and transceivers, so the networking leg gets more capacity even as the accelerator leg gets a share trim.** **One BOM datapoint that sharpens an existing bear: Fubon puts HBM4 at US$31-32/GB for NVDA but US$35-36/GB "for other GPU and custom-ASIC projects," i.e. HBM cost roughly doubling in 2027 — AVGO sits on the expensive side of that ~US$4/GB spread, which is a quantified worsening of the HBM-pass-through GM headwind already logged in Risks and in the Anthropic ASP work (~$2.5k of HBM in a ~$12k TPU ASP).** **Second source, one line only and deliberately not inflated: MS TMT webcast (07-28) — Joe Moore's rank order across his whole coverage is NVDA > AVGO > MU > SNDK, "nothing else that's wildly compelling," on the argument the four are "so much cheaper than every other name that has some speculative potential." Two supporting arguments now carried in the body: (i) **leading-edge logic without EUV depends on 2x/4x multiple-patterning ArFi**, where tools behind on **productivity, overlay and total cost of ownership** produce materially lower yields and higher cost per die — so counting units overstates substitution; (ii) the comparative point this page lacked — **[[AMAT]], [[LRCX]] and [[KLAC]] already compete with AMEC, Naura and ACM Research in China today and trade in line with or at a premium to ASML**, which holds a de facto monopoly on EUV and ArFi. **Two independent challenges to the underlying report were logged and both are flagged rather than adopted:** @jukan05 asserts The Information's story **rests on remarks by a professor at a Chinese Jiaotong university in an internal June meeting** (⚠️ unverified provenance claim — but if accurate, the evidentiary base for a ~13% drawdown is thin), and @deedydas names the vendor as **Yuliangsheng, tied to Huawei and SiCarrier, delivering to [[SMIC]], Hua Hong and CXMT** (⚠️ vendor name and Huawei linkage **not corroborated in the broker flow this ingest**; note it does not contradict the ~5/~20-unit arithmetic already on the page). **The best technical rebuttal came from outside the broker set and is worth keeping as a standing frame: @lithos_graphein defines the entry bar as a hard specification — "4.5nm MMO & >250 wph" — and offers the decisive natural experiment: Nikon is technically comparable and double-digit percent cheaper, and is shipping ZERO units this year**, because the binding constraint is **matched machine overlay**, not price or basic build capability (his published spec range for a 28nm immersion newcomer: floor **XT:1900i**, ceiling **NXT:1965Ci**). **Net read carried into the synthesis: nothing in this ingest changes the volume arithmetic already on the page (~5/~20 units vs the 131 immersion systems ASML shipped last year, qualification "many months or longer" away, domestic EUV still prototype); what changed is the price.** Also logged from the same BofA note (not ASML-specific, kept for the read-across): after a fireside with STM management, BofA's conviction on the data-centre story is rising — demand ahead of supply, capacity expanding at **Crolles 2 and Agrate**, and STM's **>$2bn 2028 data-centre target "increasingly appears conservative."** Cross-filed [[AMAT]], [[LRCX]], [[KLAC]], [[SMIC]], [themes/china-export](themes/china-export.md), [themes/semicap-wfe](themes/semicap-wfe.md). |

So this is not primarily an MI-series allocation win — it is the server-CPU/Venice leg, which lines up with the >$120B-by-2030 server-CPU TAM raise already on this page rather than with the accelerator story.** **Split, net-new: Fubon's Figure 5 has AMD at only 7.2% (2026) → 7.0% (2027) of TSMC CoWoS = 84,300 → 132,300 wafers. Against a 300k total allocation, roughly 170k wafers — well over half of AMD's 2027 packaging — sits at ASE, outside TSMC CoWoS entirely.** ⚠️ **Unit-basis guardrail recorded: 132,300 is TSMC-only CoWoS and 300k is TSMC + ASE; the two must not be netted, and neither is comparable to NVDA's 945,000 TSMC-CoWoS wafers without also noting NVDA's own 1.03mn total (TSMC + SPIL). This is the same class of basis error the UBS package-vs-die note caused across the wiki on 07-27.** **Field context that frames how good this is: in the same revision [[NVDA]] holds flat at 50.0%, [[AVGO]] is trimmed to 22.3% (from Fubon's own 24–25%), and AMD and [[MEDIATEK]] are the only two customers taking incremental 2027 allocation. Fubon also names ASE its top pick "due to its higher exposure in Vera and Venice" and is explicitly more confident in the CPU / SPE / TPU chains than in the [[NVDA]] GPU chain — i.e. the house preference sits squarely on the leg AMD is gaining in.** **One cost item logged against the ramp: HBM4 runs US$31-32/GB for NVDA but US$35-36/GB for "other GPU and custom-ASIC projects," roughly doubling HBM cost in 2027 — AMD is on the expensive side of a ~US$4/GB spread, compounding the ASE-quote (+20%) and BT-substrate (+20-30%) inflation logged on 07-27.** Cross-filed [[TSM]], [[AVGO]], [[NVDA]], [[MEDIATEK]], [themes/cowos-packaging](themes/cowos-packaging.md). |

NOTHING SUPERSEDED — MS Overweight / PT $360 (07-14) stands; no PT, rating or estimate was changed on the webcast.** **The reason this is worth a Debate bullet rather than just a log row is that it is neither a bull nor a bear datapoint — it is a DECOMPOSITION OF THE RALLY from the house that is Overweight.** Woodring: Apple is **"the antithesis of memory"** and **"the anti-memory, anti-AI-capex spend"** name making new highs, and **"maybe 80 or 90% of the move that we've seen has been kind of FACTOR AND POSITIONING AND THEMATICS-BASED as opposed to truly fundamental."** That squares with, and gives a magnitude to, the page's own scattered evidence for the same thing — the 07-15 defensive **+4%** during the semi/AI momentum unwind, and the Barclays desk using AAPL as a **low-vol funding short vs semis** (05-14/06-08). **He is emphatically NOT bearish on the business: revenue growth 15-18% and EPS growth 15-20% for the June and September quarters, "one of Apple's strongest years frankly in a number of years."** **The caution is entirely valuation and setup: the stock "has rerated to 330. That's not just for fun, that's because… they're seeing the pain of what's happening to their BILL OF MATERIALS FROM MEMORY COSTS. And so the September quarter is one with which two months of the quarter, you don't have any iPhone pricing hikes. And yet you still have bill-of-materials hikes before you get the new model launches towards the very end of the quarter."** ⚠️ **CONTRADICTION FLAGGED, NOT SMOOTHED: this lands ~24 hours after GS/Ng (07-27) modelled a BEAT ($1.93 vs $1.89) with Services held up by iCloud+/AppleCare+ offsetting App Store. Two houses, one day apart, on opposite sides of the same two lines (Services and September GM) into the same print. Both are logged; neither is averaged.** **The conclusion is a genuine two-hander and is preserved verbatim in the row: "you have to have a PERFECT REPORT," any services miss or soft GM guide is "a TOUGH T+1" — yet the name still "OUTPERFORMS INTO THE iPHONE LAUNCH" ~1.5 months out, so "you are in the SWEET SPOT FOR OWNING APPLE, but this is just maybe a bit more of a tougher setup given where Apple trades today."** Housekeeping: intra-quarter window header extended from "Apr 30 → Jul 22" to "Apr 30 → Jul 28" (rows already ran past it). | 2026-07-28 | ⭐ AAPL | **/wiki-ingest (Outlook). 1 intra-quarter row. No rating/PT superseded** (BofA Buy $380 reiterated). **Apple formally launched “Apple Upgrade” in the US** — a hardware-**leasing** platform spanning iPhone, Watch, Mac and iPad, sold through the online store, the Apple Store app and retail, and **financed via Klarna**; 12/24-month terms on iPhone and Watch, 24/36-month on Mac and iPad, from **$17.99/mo (iPhone)**. **The mechanism is the point, and it is a genuine change to how Apple captures value from the installed base rather than a new financing SKU.** BofA's articulation: under purchase/installment, Apple monetises the device fully up front but **the customer owns the residual**, which Apple only recaptures if the customer elects to trade in; under a lease, the customer pays less per month and **returns the device**, so **Apple monetises the residual itself**. The net incremental is therefore **lease payments + returned-device value + a faster replacement cycle**, with secondary benefits from **lower affordability friction, premium mix, and greater direct control of the customer relationship** — the last being strategically consistent with Apple's long-running push to disintermediate carriers. BofA is careful that the underlying mechanics (trade-ins, monthly financing, the iPhone Upgrade Program) are not new; **what is new is packaging them into one platform across all four major hardware categories.** Logged as additive product/strategy evidence for the installed-base-monetisation leg of the thesis; **no estimate or guidance impact quantified by the note, and none inferred here.** |
